#3730. Nopales Benefits: Why You Might Add Cactus to Your Diet

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Key takeaways

  • Nopales, the edible pads of the prickly pear cactus, contain antioxidants like ascorbic acid and phenolics that may help fight inflammation in your body.
  • Research suggests nopales may help manage blood sugar levels, though evidence is mixed. A small study found lower blood sugar after eating nopales with a high-carb meal, but larger reviews note insufficient evidence for diabetes prevention.
  • You can eat fresh nopales by boiling, grilling, or sautéing them, drink prickly pear juice, or take supplements containing 500 to 650 milligrams per day. Some people may experience mild nausea, diarrhea, or fullness when starting out.

Looking at a cactus with its threatening spikes and tough skin, you might not immediately think, “Yep, that’s a thing I could eat.” But guess what? Some forms of cacti are edible, tasty, and quite good for you!

Check out why it might be a good idea to pal around with nopales, the pads of the prickly pear cactus.

What is a nopal cactus?

The nopal cactus, also known as the prickly pear or the opuntia, is a shrub-like, flowering cactus native to parts of Mexico, Arizona, the Mediterranean, Australia, and other desert regions. It might be recognized by its multitude of connected pads with flowering blooms. (A pad in bloom looks a little like a flat, green paw.)

Nopales flowers are filled with a viscous, seeded substance often used in jams, jellies, pies, cakes, and other sweets. But the stems, leaves, and entire pad (with spines removed, obvs) are also edible. Traditional Mexican preparations include boiling, grilling, and sautéing.

Benefits of nopales cactus

Science is still examining the possible benefits of nopales, so some touted wellness perks are fairly tentative. Here’s a look at what we know about these desert fruits.

Has anti-inflammatory properties

Almost any time you see bright colors in edible fruits, it’s a sign they contain antioxidants. This is def true for Nopales!

Older studies have found that the fruit inside their flowers is rich in antioxidants like ascorbic acid, phenolics, and a mixture of betacyanin and betaxanthin. These compounds help fight inflammation by kicking harmful free radicals out of your cells.

Might improve joint mobility

Since they combat inflammation, the antioxidants in prickly pear could improve joint mobility. In a 2020 study, people who drank 3 ounces of prickly pear juice daily for 8 weeks had better range of motion and greater ease with daily activities than a control group who drank a placebo.

Could help manage blood sugar

Research suggests nopales may help steady blood sugar. A 2014 study found that people with type 2 diabetes who ate nopales with a high-carb breakfast had significantly lower blood sugar and insulin levels compared to those who didn’t. However, this study only included 14 participants.

In 2019, a larger systematic review assessed the fruit’s potential for blood sugar regulation. While it found insufficient evidence to support using nopales to reduce the risk of type 2 diabetes, the authors noted it might significantly reduce blood glucose levels.

Could support liver health

Showing your liver some love might involve adding nopales (or a glass of nopal juice) to your diet. According to research from 2023, this has to do with prickly pear’s effects on triglycerides. By helping reduce triglyceride accumulation, nopales could relieve stress on the liver.

Contains electrolytes

Whether you’ve overdone it on booze or are down with a stomach bug, it’s important to rehydrate with a drink that contains electrolytes. Consider prickly pear juice as your back-on-the-wagon beverage. It’s got potassium, magnesium, and calcium to help rebalance your electrolyte levels.

Forms and doses of nopal cactus

If you can track down fresh nopales where you live, cooking it yourself is a fun (if somewhat complicated) way to take advantage of its benefits. If not, look for prickly pear products, such as juices, being sure to check for added sugars.

You can also take a supplement. Nopales supplements typically contain 500-650 milligrams (mg) per day.

Nopal cactus side effects

While a prickly pear smoothie or nopales salad can be a healthy addition to your diet, consuming this cactus fruit may have some less-than-desirable side effects, like:

  • nausea
  • mild diarrhea
  • feeling full

If you’re new to nopales, take it slow at first to see how your body responds.

Takeaway

Don’t let the “prickly” in prickly pear keep you away from this desert gem. When prepared correctly, nopales are unique, nutritious food. Check ’em out when your fruit basket needs something a little different.

#2146. Hourly billing remains dominant among small, midsize firms, new 8am data shows

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hourly billing is significantly outpacing flat fee arrangements at small and midsize law firms, despite widespread predictions about the death of the billable hour, according to new data from professional business platform 8am. (Image from Shutterstock)

Hourly billing is significantly outpacing flat fee arrangements at small and midsize law firms, despite widespread predictions about the death of the billable hour, according to new data from professional business platform 8am.

The 8am SMB Law Billing and Payments Report, which was released Thursday, shows that 48% of these firms bill entirely by the hour, while only 10% exclusively use flat fees. The remaining 42% reportedly use a combination of hourly billing and flat fee arrangements.

“All too often, firms are making consequential decisions about how they bill and receive payments without real benchmarks to measure against,” said Tanya Hendricks, the chief product officer at 8am, in a news release. “Flat fee trends are a prime example—the industry narrative suggests many are moving in that direction, but our data shows most SMB firms have not.”

8am’s report, which analyzes billing and payment trends for the first half of 2026, also shows that small and midsize firms billed more but not because of higher hourly rates. Hourly rates remained unchanged across all firm sizes except midsize firms, and their hours spent per case remained mostly flat. However, what small and midsize firms earned per hour billed increased between 3% and 5%, while their total billings increased between 5% and 8% each month.

“With rates and hours being relatively flat, the driver for change was an improvement in how much recorded time made it onto an invoice,” according to the report.

Among 8am’s other key findings, small and midsize firms have slightly improved collections. Nearly 60% of firms had more invoices paid in full, compared to 58% the prior year. Meanwhile, 26% had fewer invoices go past due, compared to 27% last year.

“The picture that is emerging is one of firms getting more disciplined: getting paid more reliably, billing more of what they’ve already earned, and making more deliberate choices about how they get paid,” Hendricks also said.

The SMB Law Billing and Payments Report is based on billing and payment data from more than 60,000 firms that use 8am’s MyCase and LawPay platforms.

#2137. Billionaire received pardon after family company hired former Trump lawyer Alina Habba

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Alina Habba at the White House in March 2025. (Photo by Jabin Botsford/The Washington Post)

A Venezuelan billionaire was trying to win a pardon from President Donald Trump’s administration when Alina Habba, Trump’s former lawyer, was given a role as a general counsel in a company set up by the billionaire’s daughter.

The deal with Habba was worth more than $50 million over four years, according to a story by the New York Times.

Julio Herrera Velutini had faced felony bribery charges, but he ended up in a plea agreement in August 2025 for a misdemeanor campaign finance violation.

In January 2026, a few weeks after Habba started her work at the company, Trump granted Herrera a full pardon for the misdemeanor violation.

Habba was finalizing her role at the company while she was still serving as the interim U.S. attorney in New Jersey and as a senior adviser to former U.S. Attorney General Pam Bondi, according to the New York Times.

“Any insinuation that my employment—all of which has been vetted and disclosed to the DOJ—had anything to do with securing a pardon is false,” Habba said in a statement.
